          <text x="704" y="60" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10" fill="currentColor" opacity=".6">p3</text>

          <text x="746" y="52" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".7">.tmp,</text>
          <text x="746" y="65" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".7">torn</text>

          <!-- watermark line -->
          <line x1="530" y1="82" x2="530" y2="118" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar3)"/>
          <line x1="588" y1="82" x2="588" y2="118" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar3)"/>
          <line x1="646" y1="82" x2="646" y2="118" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" class="svg-strong" marker-end="url(#ar3)"/>

          <rect x="440" y="118" width="330" height="38" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.4" class="svg-strong"/>
          <text x="605" y="142" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor" class="svg-strong">ctx.checkpoint(parts=3, rows=1_500_000)</text>

          <text x="440" y="172" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".75">→ StepRow in SQLite. The only durable state resume reads.</text>

          <!-- divider -->
          <line x1="30" y1="200" x2="810" y2="200" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width=".8" opacity=".3"/>

          <text x="30" y="224" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10" letter-spacing="1" fill="currentColor" opacity=".7">AFTER A CRASH</text>

          <rect x="30" y="236" width="200" height="56" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="46" y="258" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor">read watermark</text>
          <text x="46" y="276" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10" fill="currentColor" opacity=".75">(3, 1_500_000)</text>

          <line x1="230" y1="264" x2="270" y2="264" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar3)"/>

          <rect x="270" y="236" width="230" height="56" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="286" y="258" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor">discard_from(3)</text>
          <text x="286" y="276" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10" fill="currentColor" opacity=".75">drops p3 and beyond</text>

          <line x1="500" y1="264" x2="540" y2="264" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar3)"/>

          <rect x="540" y="236" width="270" height="56" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.4" class="svg-strong"/>
          <text x="556" y="258" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor" class="svg-strong">re-scan OFFSET 1_500_000</text>
          <text x="556" y="276" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10" fill="currentColor" class="svg-strong">byte-identical output</text>
        </svg>
        <figcaption>
          Resume depends on <code>preserve_insertion_order=true</code> plus the row
          watermark, so the resumed run sees exactly the rows the interrupted one had not
          committed. Parquet writes to <code>.tmp</code> and renames so a torn part is never
          mistaken for a committed one; the DuckDB backend appends into a staging table
          tagged by part number, where <code>discard_from</code> is a DELETE.
        </figcaption>
      </figure>

      <div class="rule-note">
        <b>Non-obvious</b>
        The DuckDB backend's <code>abort()</code> deliberately does nothing. Each
        <code>write_part</code> is a single INSERT, so there is no torn part to clean up —
        and the staging table is exactly what a later resume reads. Dropping it would
        silently destroy the checkpoints that make resume possible.
      </div>

      <h3>Connection discipline <span class="h3-tag">db.py</span></h3>
      <ul>
        <li><strong>One DuckDB instance per process.</strong> Each unit of work takes a <code>cursor()</code> — an independent, thread-safe execution context sharing the catalog and buffer pool.</li>
        <li><strong>A connection holds one active result at a time.</strong> The streaming executor gives its Arrow scan its own cursor; writes issued on the same connection while streaming would silently truncate the reader.</li>
        <li><strong><code>Warehouse.ddl_lock</code></strong> serialises CREATE/DROP so two jobs cannot race.</li>
        <li><strong>The raw-SQL hatch is parsed, not regexed.</strong> <code>assert_read_only</code> uses DuckDB's own parser and requires exactly one SELECT statement, so comment tricks and stacked statements cannot slip through. It prevents writes, not filesystem reads.</li>
      </ul>

      <h3>Version lifecycle</h3>
      <p>Three storage properties dictate the semantics of revert and delete: version numbers are never reused and every step records the number it wrote; a version <em>owns</em> its bytes, so two version rows cannot share a <code>StoredRef</code>; history is append-only. Therefore:</p>

      <div class="tw">
        <table>
          <thead><tr><th>Operation</th><th>Behaviour</th><th>Why</th></tr></thead>
          <tbody>
            <tr>
              <td><strong>Revert</strong></td>
              <td class="wrap">Copies the old version forward as a <em>new</em> version, as a job. Column metadata copied, not recomputed.</td>
              <td class="wrap">"Revert as a new commit." Moving <code>latest_version</code> backwards would point the next write at a number some step's provenance already claims. Re-profiling would lose pins and import warnings, which cannot be recomputed at all.</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td><strong>Delete version</strong></td>
              <td class="wrap">Frees one version's bytes. Refuses the only version and the current one.</td>
              <td class="wrap">A dataset with no data is a ghost; the current version is what every query resolves to and is the high-water mark. Restore-then-delete reaches every state.</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td><strong>Delete dataset</strong></td>
              <td class="wrap">Frees disk via <code>drop_dataset</code>; 409 if it has children, unless <code>?cascade=true</code>; refuses to race a live job.</td>
              <td class="wrap">The catalog only knows about rows. Deleting metadata and leaving bytes is how a lake grows forever while the dataset list says nothing is there.</td>
            </tr>
          </tbody>
        </table>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- ================= 4 ================= -->
    <section id="datasets">
      <div class="sec-head"><span class="sec-num">04</span><h2>Defining a dataset</h2></div>

<pre>DatasetRow      id, name, kind, description, source_uri, view_sql, latest_version
   └── <b>VersionRow</b>      dataset_id, version, row_count, stored_ref, columns_schema,
        │              produced_by_step
        └── <b>ColumnRow</b>  position, name, physical_type, semantic_type, confidence,
                       role, pinned, stats, candidates, warning

JobRow          status, progress, logs, error, cancel_requested
   └── <b>StepRow</b>  op, plugin_id, plugin_version, params, inputs, outputs,
                parts_committed, rows_committed, cost, status, error</pre>

      <h3>Resolution is a single choke point</h3>
      <p>
        Nothing above the storage layer knows about paths or table names.
        <code>AppContext.resolve_source</code> is the only translator:
      </p>
<pre>resolve_source(dataset_id, version) -&gt; ResolvedSource(sql=..., columns={name: physical_type})</pre>
      <p>
        It prefers the materialised version's <code>StoredRef</code>, falls back to the
        dataset's <code>view_sql</code>, and raises otherwise. The query compiler, the
        transform runtime's join resolver and the join op all go through it — which is why a
        plugin can name a dataset id and get back something joinable without ever seeing a
        filesystem.
      </p>

      <h3>Three facts about a column, tracked apart</h3>

      <figure>
        <svg viewBox="0 0 800 218" role="img" aria-label="One VARCHAR column holding a date string carries three separate facts: an immutable physical type of VARCHAR, an editable semantic type of time.timestamp, and a role of dimension that only becomes time once the column is parsed.">
          <defs>
            <marker id="ar4" viewBox="0 0 10 10" refX="9" refY="5" markerWidth="7" markerHeight="7" orient="auto-start-reverse">
              <path d="M0 0 L10 5 L0 10 z" fill="currentColor"/>
            </marker>
          </defs>

          <rect x="250" y="12" width="300" height="42" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.6"/>
          <text x="400" y="31" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="11" fill="currentColor">column "logon_time"</text>
          <text x="400" y="46" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10" fill="currentColor" opacity=".7">'03/07/2011 08:07:29'</text>

          <line x1="330" y1="54" x2="150" y2="88" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar4)"/>
          <line x1="400" y1="54" x2="400" y2="88" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar4)"/>
          <line x1="470" y1="54" x2="650" y2="88" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar4)"/>

          <rect x="30" y="90" width="240" height="80" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="46" y="112" font-family="IBM Plex Sans Condensed, sans-serif" font-size="12.5" font-weight="700" fill="currentColor">physical type</text>
          <text x="46" y="132" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="11" fill="currentColor">VARCHAR</text>
          <text x="46" y="152" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" class="svg-strong">set at import · immutable</text>

          <rect x="280" y="90" width="240" height="80" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="296" y="112" font-family="IBM Plex Sans Condensed, sans-serif" font-size="12.5" font-weight="700" fill="currentColor">semantic type</text>
          <text x="296" y="132" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="11" fill="currentColor">time.timestamp</text>
          <text x="296" y="152" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".75">detected · editable forever</text>

          <rect x="530" y="90" width="240" height="80" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="546" y="112" font-family="IBM Plex Sans Condensed, sans-serif" font-size="12.5" font-weight="700" fill="currentColor">role</text>
          <text x="546" y="132" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="11" fill="currentColor">dimension</text>
          <text x="546" y="152" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".75">what it can do <tspan font-style="italic">as stored</tspan></text>

          <path d="M650 170 L650 194 L400 194" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" stroke-dasharray="4 3" class="svg-strong" marker-end="url(#ar4)"/>
          <text x="140" y="198" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" class="svg-strong">becomes "time" only after normalize.timestamp writes a real TIMESTAMP</text>
        </svg>
        <figcaption>
          Without the role/meaning split, every time-based plugin picked this column up and
          failed inside DuckDB with <code>No function matches -(VARCHAR, INTERVAL)</code>,
          naming neither the column nor the remedy. With it, parsing the column is the top
          suggestion, carrying the format already detected.
        </figcaption>
      </figure>

      <h3>The semantic hierarchy</h3>
      <p>
        Roots are <code>numeric</code>, <code>text</code>, <code>temporal</code>,
        <code>boolean</code>; <code>categorical</code> descends from <code>text</code>; then
        families like <code>geo.*</code>, <code>time.*</code>, <code>net.*</code>,
        <code>identity.*</code>, <code>money.*</code>. Dotted ids are a naming
        <em>convention</em>, not a structure — <code>geo.lat</code>'s parent is
        <code>numeric</code>. Matching is by ancestry, which is what makes join and chart
        suggestion automatic rather than hand-wired: two datasets are joinable when they
        share a <em>meaning</em>, not merely a column name.
      </p>

      <div class="keynote">
        <b>Pinning</b>
        A human edit sets <code>pinned=True</code>, which freezes the column against
        re-detection on every subsequent version. Accepting a proposed value is <em>not</em>
        an override — only actual changes pin, or the marker in the UI would mean nothing.
      </div>

      <p>
        <strong>Custom types.</strong> No detector will ever recognise a fleet's machine
        names, and a column with no meaning satisfies no plugin's <code>Accepts</code> gate
        and joins to nothing. So people define types, persisted in the catalog and loaded
        into the registry at context build. They are ordinary members of the hierarchy —
        they name a parent, inherit matching, and are indistinguishable to every consumer.
        Only two things separate them: they can be edited and deleted, and no detector
        produces them.
      </p>

      <h3>The derivation tree</h3>
      <p>
        The catalog already records a DAG in <code>Step.inputs</code>/<code>outputs</code>.
        Two rules turn the dataset-creating part of it into a tree the UI can nest:
      </p>
      <ul>
        <li><strong>A transform is not an edge.</strong> It produces a new <em>version</em> of the dataset it was given, so it belongs to that dataset's history, not to its offspring.</li>
        <li><strong>A join has two parents but a node has one.</strong> It nests under its left input and names the other parent inline, so the second edge stays visible.</li>
      </ul>
      <p>A dataset whose parent was deleted surfaces as a root rather than disappearing.</p>
    </section>

    <!-- ================= 5 ================= -->
    <section id="pipeline">
      <div class="sec-head"><span class="sec-num">05</span><h2>Import &amp; processing</h2></div>

      <p>
        A column's physical type is settled the moment the file is read, is copied into
        every record, and can never be changed afterwards — storage is immutable per version,
        and the only escape is a transform that <em>adds</em> a column beside it. Meaning and
        role are worked out later and stay editable forever.
      </p>

      <div class="keynote">
        <b>The asymmetry that shaped import</b>
        The one decision that could not be revisited was the one nobody was shown. So import
        proposes a plan first — and the proposal is built by running the <em>real</em>
        profiler over a sample rather than reimplementing it, which is the property that
        makes it worth trusting: what it shows is what the import will do, because it is the
        same code.
      </div>

      <figure>
        <svg viewBox="0 0 840 380" role="img" aria-label="Import runs in two phases: a plan phase that samples 2000 rows and proposes a per-column type, format, meaning and role; then the import itself, which holds ambiguous date columns as text, casts inside a projection rather than at read time, and profiles what was written.">
          <defs>
            <marker id="ar5" viewBox="0 0 10 10" refX="9" refY="5" markerWidth="7" markerHeight="7" orient="auto-start-reverse">
              <path d="M0 0 L10 5 L0 10 z" fill="currentColor"/>
            </marker>
          </defs>

          <text x="30" y="20" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10" letter-spacing="1" fill="currentColor" opacity=".7">PHASE 1 — PROPOSE</text>

          <rect x="30" y="32" width="150" height="46" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="105" y="52" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor">file on disk</text>
          <text x="105" y="67" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".7">browse-root confined</text>

          <line x1="180" y1="55" x2="216" y2="55" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar5)"/>

          <rect x="216" y="32" width="180" height="46" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="306" y="52" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor">sample 2,000 rows</text>
          <text x="306" y="67" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".7">the real profiler</text>

          <line x1="396" y1="55" x2="432" y2="55" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar5)"/>

          <text x="739" y="52" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor">user edits</text>
          <text x="739" y="67" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".7">or just presses Import</text>

          <!-- ambiguity settle -->
          <path d="M306 78 L306 108 L532 108 L532 90" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.1" stroke-dasharray="4 3" marker-end="url(#ar5)"/>
          <text x="316" y="124" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">if day-first vs month-first is unsettled, scan 200k rows (~109ms) and ask which reading fails</text>

          <line x1="30" y1="146" x2="810" y2="146" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width=".8" opacity=".3"/>
          <text x="30" y="168" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10" letter-spacing="1" fill="currentColor" opacity=".7">PHASE 2 — IMPORT (a job)</text>

          <rect x="30" y="182" width="170" height="52" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="46" y="203" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor">Reader → relation</text>
          <text x="46" y="221" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".75">DuckDB streams it</text>

          <line x1="200" y1="208" x2="236" y2="208" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar5)"/>

          <text x="252" y="203" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor" class="svg-strong">re-read ambiguous cols</text>
          <text x="252" y="221" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" class="svg-strong">as VARCHAR</text>

          <line x1="436" y1="208" x2="472" y2="208" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar5)"/>

          <rect x="472" y="182" width="180" height="52" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="488" y="203" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor">measure cast losses</text>
          <text x="488" y="221" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".75">per column, before write</text>

          <line x1="562" y1="234" x2="562" y2="264" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar5)"/>

          <rect x="472" y="264" width="180" height="52" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="488" y="285" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor">write via projection</text>
          <text x="488" y="303" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".75">try_cast → NULL, never drop</text>

          <line x1="472" y1="290" x2="436" y2="290" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar5)"/>

          <rect x="236" y="264" width="200" height="52" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="252" y="285" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor">re-derive schema</text>
          <text x="252" y="303" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".75">from what was written</text>

          <line x1="236" y1="290" x2="200" y2="290" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar5)"/>

          <rect x="30" y="264" width="170" height="52" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="46" y="285" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor">version + profile</text>
          <text x="46" y="303" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".75">detectors vote</text>

          <text x="30" y="346" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">warnings attached to columns: sniffer date ambiguity · cast losses · rejected rows</text>
          <text x="30" y="362" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">on any failure, the dataset row created at the start is deleted again — no ghosts</text>
        </svg>
        <figcaption>
          Casting happens in the projection that materialises the version, not at read time.
          At read time it cannot be survived: <code>types={'date':'TIMESTAMP'}</code> aborts
          the whole import on the first unparseable row, and <code>ignore_errors</code>
          answers that by silently dropping rows. A projection cannot drop anything, so a
          value that will not convert becomes NULL and the count is reported.
        </figcaption>
      </figure>

      <h3>Profiling <span class="h3-tag">roughly constant-time, 10 MB or 10 GB</span></h3>
      <ul>
        <li><strong>Counts come from the whole table</strong>, not the sample. Sampled distinct counts are capped at the sample size, so any column with more distinct values than that looks like a per-row identifier — and "roughly as many distinct values as rows" is exactly the test that tells a user id from an event id. A 40,000-user column sampled at 20,000 rows would be read as a primary key and excluded from everything. <code>approx_count_distinct</code> is HyperLogLog: 11.4M rows × 19 columns in ~0.3s.</li>
        <li><strong>Only sample values and top values stay sampled</strong>, because those genuinely need rows rather than aggregates.</li>
        <li><strong>Detectors vote.</strong> The highest-confidence guess above 0.5 wins; the rest are kept as <code>candidates</code>; a pinned column is never challenged.</li>
      </ul>

      <h3>Dates, and what refuses to be guessed</h3>
      <p>
        DuckDB's sniffer types anything non-ISO as VARCHAR, so <code>Mar 04, 2016</code>
        arrives as text and stays unusable for bucketing or charting. About 35 formats are
        tried against sampled values and ranked by how many parse, so parsing needs no format
        argument. What the module refuses to do is guess when guessing is unsafe:
      </p>
      <ul>
        <li>A <strong>text</strong> column that reads both ways is reported ambiguous, and parsing fails naming both options. Any day past the 12th in the sample resolves it.</li>
        <li>When a 2,000-row prefix cannot choose — a time-sorted export's first rows can span one day, where every day is ≤ 12 — the file is asked how often each reading actually fails over 200,000 rows.</li>
        <li>A column DuckDB <strong>already converted</strong> is worse: the sniffer picked a reading and did not record which. So the raw file is re-read at import and a warning names both readings, the one taken, and the <code>timestampformat</code> that pins the other.</li>
        <li>Numeric columns named like times are checked against epoch ranges, so <code>event_time</code> as a BIGINT is recognised rather than averaged.</li>
      </ul>

      <h3>The five operations</h3>
      <p>
        <code>POST /api/operations</code> is the single request shape for every
        data-producing call, and returns 202 with a job id.
      </p>
      <div class="tw">
        <table>
          <thead><tr><th>op</th><th>Input → output</th><th>Note</th></tr></thead>
          <tbody>
            <tr><td><code>import</code></td><td>URI → new source dataset @ v1</td><td class="wrap">Two-phase, above</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>transform</code></td><td>dataset v<em>n</em> → same dataset v<em>n+1</em></td><td class="wrap">Normalize / extract / annotate; the mode decides how it runs</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>aggregate</code></td><td>dataset → <strong>new</strong> aggregate dataset</td><td class="wrap">Changes cardinality, so it cannot be a new version. Also accepts <code>from_query</code> — materialising a chart's own QuerySpec</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>join</code></td><td>two datasets → <strong>new</strong> join dataset</td><td class="wrap">Composite key; fan-out refused unless <code>allow_fanout</code></td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>revert</code></td><td>dataset v<em>k</em> → same dataset v<em>n+1</em></td><td class="wrap">Copies bytes forward; metadata copied, not recomputed</td></tr>
          </tbody>
        </table>
      </div>

      <h3>Job lifecycle</h3>
<pre>submit_operation ──▶ JobRow(queued) + StepRow(queued)
                          │
     ThreadPoolJobRunner ─┴─▶ JobRow(running), StepRow(running)
                                    │
              JobCtx: log() · progress() · check_cancelled() · checkpoint()
                                    │
              ┌─────────────────────┼──────────────────────┐
        <b>succeeded</b>              <b>cancelled</b>                <b>failed</b>
                          (JobCancelled)          (message on the job row)
                                    │
                          <b>BudgetExceeded</b> → succeeded + "stopped early"
                          <i>(partial results are committed on purpose)</i></pre>

      <p>
        <code>JobRunner</code> is a Protocol; the v0 thread pool can be swapped for
        RQ/Celery/Temporal without touching a plugin. A thread pool is the right shape
        because the work is either inside DuckDB (which releases the GIL) or awaiting network
        I/O. Progress is throttled to 4&nbsp;Hz so a fast batch loop does not hammer SQLite,
        and streams to the UI over SSE.
      </p>

      <h3>Guardrails</h3>
      <p>
        The failure mode this codebase designs against is <strong>a job that succeeds and is
        wrong</strong>. Every guard below exists because some silent failure was cheaper to
        detect than to discover downstream.
      </p>

      <div class="tw">
        <table>
          <thead><tr><th>Guard</th><th>Catches</th></tr></thead>
          <tbody>
            <tr><td class="wrap"><code>ParseCheck</code> on a <code>SqlPlan</code></td><td><code>try_cast</code>/<code>try_strptime</code> return NULL on failure, so a wrong format <em>succeeds</em> and writes a column of NULLs. Measured over a 20k-row sample <strong>before writing</strong>; the error names the rate, an example value, and formats that would have worked. An all-null input is not blamed on the transform.</td></tr>
            <tr><td class="wrap">Cast-loss counting</td><td>Values a planned import cast turns into NULL — reported per column, never silent</td></tr>
            <tr><td class="wrap">Right-side uniqueness</td><td>A left join onto duplicated keys multiplies rows instead of annotating them</td></tr>
            <tr><td class="wrap">Match-rate probe</td><td>A key matching nothing still reports success; a left join answers "no match" with NULL</td></tr>
            <tr><td class="wrap">Name collision</td><td>DuckDB returns two result columns of the same name without complaint; the catalog stores one column per name per version</td></tr>
            <tr><td class="wrap">Fan-out refusal</td><td>Result rows &gt; left rows without <code>allow_fanout</code> — the written result is dropped</td></tr>
            <tr><td class="wrap">Limit-truncation refusal</td><td>An aggregate that stopped exactly on its limit is truncated and incomplete; <code>limited_on_purpose</code> distinguishes a deliberate top-K</td></tr>
            <tr><td class="wrap">Ghost-dataset cleanup</td><td>A dataset row created before the work that fills it, when that work fails</td></tr>
          </tbody>
        </table>
      </div>

      <div class="rule-note">
        <b>Check first, guard last</b>
        Everything the join op's post-hoc guard checks is also checked <em>before</em> the
        write by the join-preview service — so the join form reports uniqueness, match rate
        and collisions live as the key is edited, for the price of a GROUP BY and a bounded
        probe, instead of a full pass that arrives as a failed job.
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- ================= 6 ================= -->
    <section id="plugins">
      <div class="sec-head"><span class="sec-num">06</span><h2>Plugin architecture</h2></div>

      <p>
        Plugins are <strong>heterogeneous in contract, homogeneous in execution</strong>.
        Each declares two independent things: a <code>kind</code>, which fixes its Python
        interface, and a <code>mode</code>, which fixes its scheduling and the facilities the
        runtime hands it.
      </p>

      <figure>
        <svg viewBox="0 0 820 330" role="img" aria-label="One Transform kind fans out into three execution modes — pushdown emitting SQL, batch processing Arrow record batches, and external calling an LLM through an async pool — and all three converge on one identical output contract: a new dataset version.">
          <defs>
            <marker id="ar6" viewBox="0 0 10 10" refX="9" refY="5" markerWidth="7" markerHeight="7" orient="auto-start-reverse">
              <path d="M0 0 L10 5 L0 10 z" fill="currentColor"/>
            </marker>
          </defs>

          <rect x="290" y="14" width="240" height="46" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.8"/>
          <text x="410" y="35" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Sans Condensed, sans-serif" font-size="14" font-weight="700" fill="currentColor">Transform</text>
          <text x="410" y="51" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".75">one kind — normalize, extract, annotate</text>

          <line x1="340" y1="60" x2="150" y2="96" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar6)"/>
          <line x1="410" y1="60" x2="410" y2="96" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar6)"/>
          <line x1="480" y1="60" x2="670" y2="96" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar6)"/>

          <rect x="30" y="98" width="240" height="128" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="46" y="120" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="11" fill="currentColor" class="svg-strong">mode="pushdown"</text>
          <text x="46" y="140" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor">sql(ctx) → SqlPlan</text>
          <line x1="46" y1="150" x2="254" y2="150" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width=".7" opacity=".3"/>
          <text x="46" y="168" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">runtime assembles SELECT</text>
          <text x="46" y="184" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">enforces ParseChecks</text>
          <text x="46" y="200" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">widens with a JoinPlan</text>
          <text x="46" y="216" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">one DuckDB statement</text>

          <rect x="290" y="98" width="240" height="128" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="306" y="120" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="11" fill="currentColor" class="svg-strong">mode="batch"</text>
          <text x="306" y="140" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor">process(batch) → batch</text>
          <line x1="306" y1="150" x2="514" y2="150" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width=".7" opacity=".3"/>
          <text x="306" y="168" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">streamed Arrow, own cursor</text>
          <text x="306" y="184" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">part-file checkpointing</text>
          <text x="306" y="200" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">resume from watermark</text>
          <text x="306" y="216" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">progress, cancellation</text>

          <rect x="550" y="98" width="240" height="128" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2"/>
          <text x="566" y="120" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="11" fill="currentColor" class="svg-strong">mode="external"</text>
          <text x="566" y="140" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="10.5" fill="currentColor">async process_rows(...)</text>
          <line x1="566" y1="150" x2="774" y2="150" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width=".7" opacity=".3"/>
          <text x="566" y="168" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">persistent result cache</text>
          <text x="566" y="184" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">bounded pool, retries</text>
          <text x="566" y="200" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">cost cap, injected client</text>
          <text x="566" y="216" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" opacity=".8">row-level failure isolation</text>

          <line x1="150" y1="226" x2="340" y2="262" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar6)"/>
          <line x1="410" y1="226" x2="410" y2="262" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar6)"/>
          <line x1="670" y1="226" x2="480" y2="262" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#ar6)"/>

          <rect x="250" y="264" width="320" height="48" rx="3" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.8" class="svg-strong"/>
          <text x="410" y="285" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Sans Condensed, sans-serif" font-size="13.5" font-weight="700" fill="currentColor" class="svg-strong">a new DatasetVersion</text>
          <text x="410" y="302" text-anchor="middle" font-family="IBM Plex Mono, monospace" font-size="9.5" fill="currentColor" class="svg-strong">identical contract — nothing downstream can tell which ran</text>
        </svg>
        <figcaption>
          The stage a plugin belongs to does not determine how it runs. An extractor may be a
          cheap regex (<code>pushdown</code>) or an LLM call (<code>external</code>);
          versioning, profiling, the UI and the agent never learn which path executed.
        </figcaption>
      </figure>

      <h3>The six kinds</h3>
      <div class="tw">
        <table>
          <thead><tr><th>Kind</th><th>Interface</th><th>Default mode</th></tr></thead>
          <tbody>
            <tr><td><code>Reader</code></td><td>URI → DuckDB relation</td><td><code>pushdown</code></td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>Detector</code></td><td>column stats → semantic guesses</td><td><code>inspect</code></td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>Transform</code></td><td>DatasetVersion → DatasetVersion</td><td>any of three</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>Aggregator</code></td><td>dataset → query plan → new aggregate dataset</td><td><code>pushdown</code></td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>Suggester</code></td><td>catalog context → executable suggestions</td><td><code>inspect</code></td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>Visualizer</code></td><td>dataset → <code>VizSpec</code></td><td><code>inspect</code></td></tr>
          </tbody>
        </table>
      </div>
      <p>
        A <code>Reader</code> returns a <em>relation</em>, not Arrow batches, so DuckDB
        streams a 10&nbsp;GB file itself instead of round-tripping it through Python.
        <code>Aggregator</code> is a separate kind from <code>Transform</code> only because
        it changes cardinality. <code>inspect</code>-mode plugins run in the request thread
        and never create a job.
      </p>

      <h3>The descriptor is the single source of truth</h3>

<pre>@register
<b>class</b> NormalizeTimestamp(Transform):
    id       = "normalize.timestamp"
    title    = "Parse timestamp"
    mode     = "pushdown"
    version  = "1"
    Params   = TimestampParams          <i># one Pydantic model</i>
    accepts  = Accepts(semantic_types=("time.timestamp",))
    produces = Produces(semantic_types=("time.timestamp",))

    <b>def</b> sql(self, ctx: TransformCtx) -&gt; SqlPlan: ...</pre>

      <p>
        <code>descriptor()</code> serialises exactly that, including
        <code>Params.model_json_schema()</code>. <code>GET /api/plugins</code> returns it
        verbatim, and it feeds FastAPI validation, the OpenAPI docs, the form renderer, and
        the agent's tool schema. <code>register</code> validates at import time: non-empty
        id/kind/mode/title, no duplicate ids, and every semantic type named in
        <code>accepts</code>/<code>produces</code> must exist.
      </p>

      <p>
        <code>Accepts</code> answers <em>"what can I do with this dataset?"</em> —
        <code>GET /api/plugins?applicable_to=&lt;id&gt;</code> filters on semantic types
        (descendants count), dataset kinds and minimum rows. That one query drives both the
        UI action list and the agent's choices.
      </p>

      <div class="keynote">
        <b>Extension without forking</b>
        The registry loads the <code>dataq.plugins</code> entry-point group on first lookup,
        so <code>pip install dataq-plugin-geoip</code> adds capability with no core change.
        An entry point may be a <code>Plugin</code> subclass or a
        <code>register(registry)</code> hook.
      </div>

      <h3>What the runtime provides, per mode</h3>

      <p>
        <strong>Pushdown.</strong> The plugin returns a <code>SqlPlan</code> — column
        expressions, not a query. The runtime assembles the SELECT, so a transform cannot
        accidentally drop the projection, reorder rows, or change cardinality. If a
        <code>JoinPlan</code> is declared the runtime widens the source first, wrapped in a
        subquery so the outer projection stays unqualified — which means <code>add</code>
        expressions can reference brought-across columns without knowing a join happened.
      </p>

      <p>
        <strong>External.</strong> The plugin implements only <code>process_rows</code> and
        declares <code>batch_size</code>, <code>max_concurrency</code>,
        <code>output_columns</code> and <code>cache_key_fields</code>. Everything that makes
        such a plugin survivable is supplied around it:
      </p>
      <ul>
        <li>a <strong>persistent result cache</strong> in the warehouse, keyed on <code>sha256(plugin_id, plugin_version, params, model, declared fields)</code> — so re-running an extraction over a superset of rows only pays for the new rows, and unrelated schema churn does not cause a miss. Bumping <code>version</code> invalidates it;</li>
        <li>a bounded semaphore pool, so the plugin never manages concurrency;</li>
        <li>retry with exponential backoff;</li>
        <li><strong>row-level failure isolation</strong> — a chunk that fails after three attempts lands as NULLs plus a <code>&lt;plugin&gt;_error</code> string rather than killing an hour-long job;</li>
        <li>cost accounting and a hard <code>max_cost_usd</code> cap, which the runner treats as <em>success with partial results</em>, not failure.</li>
      </ul>
      <p>The model client is injected, never constructed by the plugin, which keeps auth and model choice central and lets tests pass a fake.</p>

      <h3>Built-ins</h3>
      <div class="tw">
        <table>
          <thead><tr><th>Kind</th><th>Ids</th></tr></thead>
          <tbody>
            <tr><td>Reader</td><td class="wrap"><code>read.csv</code> · <code>read.parquet</code> · <code>read.json</code></td></tr>
            <tr><td>Detector</td><td class="wrap"><code>detect.ip</code> · <code>detect.country_iso2</code> · <code>detect.latlng</code> · <code>detect.timestamp</code> · <code>detect.email</code> · <code>detect.url</code> · <code>detect.money</code> · <code>detect.boolean</code> · <code>detect.cardinality</code> · <code>detect.share</code></td></tr>
            <tr><td>Transform</td><td class="wrap"><code>normalize.ip</code> · <code>normalize.timestamp</code> · <code>normalize.country</code> · <code>normalize.numeric</code> <em>(pushdown)</em> · <code>transform.ip_class</code> <em>(batch)</em> · <code>extract.entities</code> <em>(external)</em> · <code>enrich.features</code> <em>(pushdown)</em></td></tr>
            <tr><td>Aggregator</td><td class="wrap"><code>agg.frequency</code> · <code>agg.time_rollup</code> · <code>agg.topk</code> · <code>agg.features</code></td></tr>
            <tr><td>Suggester</td><td class="wrap"><code>suggest.viz</code> · <code>suggest.aggregate</code> · <code>suggest.normalize</code> · <code>suggest.features</code> · <code>suggest.join</code></td></tr>
            <tr><td>Visualizer</td><td class="wrap"><code>viz.histogram</code> · <code>viz.bar</code> · <code>viz.timeseries</code> · <code>viz.map_points</code> · <code>viz.table</code> · <code>viz.timeline</code></td></tr>
          </tbody>
        </table>
      </div>

      <h3>Behavioural features — the plugin pair worth reading</h3>
      <p>
        "How unusual is this event, for this actor, right now" is always the same shape:
        <em>an aggregate, over a partition, within a window, evaluated per row</em>. Written
        as a shorthand that parses to a typed <code>Feature</code> — which is what the API
        and agent exchange — and compiles to one SQL window expression:
      </p>
<pre>share()            by activity_type
count()            by user, activity_type over 30d
days_since_last()  by user, activity_type
avg(amount)        by user over 7d as spend_7d</pre>
      <p>
        It is split into two plugins because the intermediate is worth having.
        <code>agg.features</code> builds a feature <em>table</em> — one row per
        <code>(user, activity_type[, day])</code> — which is a dataset you can chart on its
        own. <code>enrich.features</code> attaches it back on a composite key and computes
        what no grouped table can hold: "the 30 days before <em>this</em> event", "days since
        the previous one".
      </p>
      <ul>
        <li><strong>Cost tracks distinct sorts, not feature count.</strong> Features sharing a window emit identical OVER text and DuckDB reuses one sort. On 5M rows: five features over one window, 0.7s; six across three windows, 6.3s.</li>
        <li><strong>A feature that sees the future says so.</strong> A trailing window only looks back, but a whole-dataset <code>share()</code> counts events after the row it describes. That is recorded on the column rather than left to be rediscovered.</li>
      </ul>
    </section>

    <!-- ================= 7 ================= -->
    <section id="query">
      <div class="sec-head"><span class="sec-num">07</span><h2>Query &amp; visualisation</h2></div>

      <p>
        The compiler enforces two rules, because specs arrive from the UI <em>and</em> from an
        LLM agent:
      </p>
      <ol class="body-list">
        <li>Every identifier is validated against the resolved source schema, then quoted. An unknown column is an error, never interpolated text.</li>
        <li>Every literal is bound as a <code>?</code> parameter, never formatted into the string.</li>
      </ol>
      <p>
        Together, a malicious or confused spec cannot inject SQL.
        <code>inline_params</code> exists purely for <em>display</em> — folding parameters
        back as quoted literals so the SQL editor can be seeded with a runnable query.
      </p>

      <p>
        <code>TimeBucket</code> has two modes: truncation (each calendar day its own bucket)
        and <code>part</code> — a <em>cyclical</em> slice where every 1pm across the dataset
        collapses into one bucket, which is how you ask for the busiest hour of the day. In
        part mode the compiler emits a readable label plus an <code>{alias}_ord</code>
        ordinal, because "Thu" sorts alphabetically and would otherwise scramble the chart.
      </p>

      <h3>The rendering contract</h3>
<pre>Visualizer ──▶ VizSpec { renderer, title, query, chart?, timeline?, spec?, animate? }
                        │
   services/inspect ────┤  binds query.dataset, runs it, resolves the chart
                        ▼
   RenderedViz { spec, data, row_count, <b>sql</b>, elapsed_ms, truncated }
                        │
   frontend RENDERERS[spec.renderer] ──▶ VegaLite | MapLibre | Table | Timeline</pre>

      <p>
        The backend never renders. Consequence: <strong>a new chart type that reuses an
        existing renderer is a backend-only change.</strong> The service — not the plugin —
        binds <code>query.dataset</code>, so a visualizer can never read from somewhere it
        was not asked to.
      </p>

      <p>
        <code>ChartSpec</code> is a deliberate typed subset of Vega-Lite's vocabulary, so it
        compiles almost 1:1. The point of owning it rather than passing Vega-Lite through is
        that it can be <em>resolved</em> against things DataQ already knows: the query's real
        output columns, and each column's semantic type — a <code>time.timestamp</code> is
        temporal, a <code>money.amount</code> quantitative, a <code>geo.country_iso2</code>
        nominal. A field the query does not return is an error rather than a silently empty
        chart.
      </p>

      <div class="keynote">
        <b>Structure, plus a documented way out</b>
        The same pairing appears three times: <code>QuerySpec</code> with its read-only raw
        SQL path, <code>ChartSpec</code> with <code>raw_vega_lite</code>, and
        <code>AggregatePlan.derive</code> for the window functions <code>QuerySpec</code>
        deliberately cannot express. The hatch is always narrower or more trusted than the
        structured path — <code>derive</code> is authored by plugin code, never by a user or
        an agent.
      </div>

      <p>
        Every chart carries the SQL that produced it, collapsed underneath — a chart chosen
        by a suggester or an agent rather than by the person reading it is hard to trust
        otherwise. Dashboards persist the <em>recipe</em> (a list of VizSpecs), not a
        snapshot.
      </p>
    </section>

    <!-- ================= 8 ================= -->
    <section id="agent">
      <div class="sec-head"><span class="sec-num">08</span><h2>The agent</h2></div>

      <p>
        Tools bind to the service layer, not to HTTP. Two permission scopes exist, and the
        split is what makes agent-backed <em>plugins</em> safe:
      </p>

      <div class="tw">
        <table>
          <thead><tr><th>Scope</th><th>Tools</th><th>Held by</th></tr></thead>
          <tbody>
            <tr>
              <td><code>read_only</code></td>
              <td class="wrap"><code>list_datasets</code> · <code>profile_dataset</code> · <code>run_query</code> · <code>get_suggestions</code> · <code>list_plugins</code> · <code>render_viz</code> · <code>get_job</code></td>
              <td class="wrap">Agent-backed plugins — so a plugin can explore data but can never spawn jobs. No recursion.</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td><code>full</code></td>
              <td class="wrap">adds <code>create_aggregate</code> · <code>create_join</code> · <code>apply_transform</code> · <code>save_dashboard</code></td>
              <td class="wrap">The chat agent, driven by a human who can see and cancel what it starts.</td>
            </tr>
          </tbody>
        </table>
      </div>

      <p>
        The loop is written out rather than using the SDK's tool runner, because each step is
        streamed to the UI as a structured <code>AgentTurn</code> and dispatch is gated on
        scope. <code>POST /api/agent/estimate</code> prices a run before it starts.
      </p>

      <p>
        Because suggestions carry executable <code>action</code> payloads, the agent and the
        UI consume them identically — one renders a button, the other calls the endpoint.
      </p>
    </section>

    <!-- ================= 9 ================= -->
    <section id="surface">
      <div class="sec-head"><span class="sec-num">09</span><h2>API &amp; frontend</h2></div>

<pre>GET  /api/plugins?kind=&amp;mode=&amp;applicable_to=    what can I do with this dataset?
GET  /api/semantic-types   POST   DELETE /{id}  the meaning vocabulary
POST /api/sources/preview | /plan | /upload     import phase 1
GET  /api/sources/browse                        server-side file picker (confined)
POST /api/operations                            import|transform|aggregate|join|revert → 202
POST /api/inspect                               synchronous twin for viz/suggesters
GET  /api/jobs  /{id}  /{id}/stream  /{id}/cancel
GET  /api/datasets  /tree  /{id}  /{id}/related  /{id}/dependents
GET  /api/datasets/{id}/profile | versions | lineage | suggestions | feature-plan
GET  /api/datasets/{id}/join-candidates
POST /api/datasets/{id}/join-preview            what would this join do? (no job)
POST /api/datasets/{id}/columns/{col}/type      pin a meaning
POST /api/datasets/{id}/revert                  → 202
DEL  /api/datasets/{id}  /{id}/versions/{n}
POST /api/query  /api/query/compile  /api/query/sql
GET/POST /api/dashboards
POST /api/auth/login    GET /api/auth/me
GET  /api/agent/tools   POST /api/agent/estimate  /api/agent/chat</pre>

      <h3>Auth</h3>
      <p>
        Auth engages only when asked for — a shared token, a user list, or
        <code>DATAQ_REQUIRE_AUTH</code>. A laptop instance nobody else can reach gets no
        login screen. Passwords are scrypt hashes; login exchanges one for a session token
        signed with a key kept in the data directory, so a restart does not sign everybody
        out. The committed built-in account deliberately does <strong>not</strong> satisfy
        <code>DATAQ_REQUIRE_AUTH</code> — its hash is public, so a deployment must bring its
        own credential or the app refuses to start.
      </p>

      <h3>Frontend</h3>
      <div class="tw">
        <table>
          <thead><tr><th>Area</th><th>Notes</th></tr></thead>
          <tbody>
            <tr><td><code>api/</code></td><td class="wrap">Typed client mirroring the backend models</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>renderers/</code></td><td class="wrap">Registry keyed on <code>VizSpec.renderer</code> — the mirror image of the plugin registry</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>SchemaForm.tsx</code></td><td class="wrap">Renders any plugin's JSON Schema as a form; unwraps Pydantic's <code>anyOf: [T, null]</code>, humanises enum values, turns column-typed fields into pickers</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>ImportPanel</code>, <code>FileBrowser</code></td><td class="wrap">The two-phase import, including the malformed-row choice</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>JoinPanel</code></td><td class="wrap">The live join preview</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>MeaningSelect</code></td><td class="wrap">Semantic-type editing, which pins</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>JobProgress</code></td><td class="wrap">SSE job feed</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>FeatureDraft</code></td><td class="wrap">The pre-filled feature expression editor</td></tr>
          </tbody>
        </table>
      </div>

      <p>
        Files are read <strong>in place</strong> by DuckDB, so the import box needs a path
        the <em>server</em> can open — which a browser's file input cannot supply, since it
        hands over contents rather than a location. Hence the server-side browser, confined
        to <code>DATAQ_BROWSE_ROOTS</code>, with upload as the fallback for a file that
        really is only on the viewer's machine. Browsing is the better path for multi-GB
        files: it avoids the copy entirely.
      </p>

      <h3>Configuration that changes architecture</h3>
      <div class="tw">
        <table>
          <thead><tr><th>Setting</th><th>Default</th><th>Effect</th></tr></thead>
          <tbody>
            <tr><td><code>DATAQ_DATA_DIR</code></td><td><code>./data</code></td><td class="wrap">Everything lives here; deployment is "mount one volume"</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>DATAQ_STORAGE</code></td><td><code>parquet</code></td><td class="wrap">Which <code>StorageBackend</code> holds dataset bytes</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>DATAQ_JOB_WORKERS</code></td><td>2</td><td class="wrap">Thread-pool size</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>DATAQ_BATCH_ROWS</code></td><td>100,000</td><td class="wrap">Arrow batch size for streaming modes</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>DATAQ_CHECKPOINT_EVERY_BATCHES</code></td><td>5</td><td class="wrap">How often a part is flushed and the watermark recorded</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>DATAQ_PROFILE_SAMPLE_ROWS</code></td><td>10,000</td><td class="wrap">Profiling sample</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>DATAQ_BROWSE_ROOTS</code></td><td>home + cwd</td><td class="wrap">What the file browser may list</td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>DATAQ_ALLOW_REMOTE_URIS</code></td><td>false</td><td class="wrap">Whether an import may name <code>s3://</code> / <code>https://</code></td></tr>
            <tr><td><code>DATAQ_STATIC_DIR</code></td><td>unset</td><td class="wrap">Serve a built SPA at <code>/</code>, so production is a single container</td></tr>
          </tbody>
        </table>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- ================= 10 ================= -->
    <section id="rules">
      <div class="sec-head"><span class="sec-num">10</span><h2>Design rules, as an index</h2></div>

      <p>Ten rules recur, and between them explain most of the code.</p>

      <ol class="rules">
        <li><strong>A plugin never sees storage.</strong> It names a dataset id; the service resolves it.</li>
        <li><strong>The runtime owns the projection</strong>, so a transform cannot change cardinality by accident.</li>
        <li><strong>Structure, plus a documented way out.</strong> The escape hatch is always narrower or more trusted than the structured path.</li>
        <li><strong>Silence is the enemy.</strong> Every operation that can succeed while being wrong has a pre-write check that names the problem in a sentence, with an example.</li>
        <li><strong>Check before writing, not after.</strong> The expensive guard stays as the last line of defence, but the same question is asked first for the price of a GROUP BY.</li>
        <li><strong>What cannot be recomputed is carried, not re-derived.</strong> Pins and import warnings survive transforms and reverts; stats do not need to.</li>
        <li><strong>The immutable decision gets shown first.</strong> Physical type is proposed with evidence before import, because it can never be revisited.</li>
        <li><strong>One definition, many consumers.</strong> A plugin's <code>Params</code>; a column's semantic type; a <code>VizSpec</code>'s renderer name.</li>
        <li><strong>Metadata and data have different write patterns</strong>, so they live in different stores.</li>
        <li><strong>A failed operation leaves the catalog as it found it.</strong></li>
      </ol>
    </section>

    <footer>
      Source of record: <code>docs/ARCHITECTURE.md</code> in the DataQ repository.
    </footer>
  </main>
</div>

Assistant2026-09-01T05:23:45.740Z

Two deliverables, same content:

docs/ARCHITECTURE.md — the source-of-record doc, next to the code it describes.

Shareable page with diagrams: https://claude.ai/code/artifact/647bbbeb-3038-4af1-8f48-ca994239a2f1

What it covers

Storage strategy — three stores, split by write pattern not size: SQLite catalog (many tiny transactional writes — job heartbeats, step status), the DuckDB warehouse (engine + external result cache), and the lake behind the StorageBackend protocol. The two backends, why DuckDB's single-writer lock and file opacity force the abstraction, and how immutability buys checkpoint/resume — with a diagram of the part-writer watermark and the crash path.

Datasets — the Dataset → Version → Column model, resolve_source as the single choke point that keeps plugins from ever seeing a path, the semantic-type hierarchy, pinning, custom types, and the two rules that turn the lineage DAG into a tree.

Import pipeline — the two-phase design and why it exists: physical type is the one decision that can never be revisited, so it gets proposed first, by running the real profiler. Then the import itself: holding ambiguous date columns as text, casting in the projection rather than at read time, re-deriving the schema from what was written, and the eight guardrails that exist because "a job that succeeds and is wrong" is the failure mode the codebase designs against.

Plugins — kind × mode as independent axes, with a diagram of one Transform fanning into three execution paths that converge on an identical output contract. The descriptor as single source of truth, entry-point extension, and what the runtime supplies per mode (notably the external-mode result cache and cost cap).

Plus the query/viz contract, the agent's two permission scopes, API surface, frontend, and a closing index of the ten recurring design rules.

Three corrections I made against the README while reading the code: the agent's full-scope transform tool is apply_transform (not run_transform), get_job is in the read-only set, and the agent's create_join exposes only a single column pair while the underlying op takes a composite key.
